## Quarterfinals Round-Up

*by Marc Calderaro*

 

### Luis Scott-Vargas (Melira Pod) vs. Michael Sigrist (Affinity)

This is a return to the Top 8 for both these players. Michael Sigrist finished in the Top 8 Pro Tour Boston on Team Work With Josh. Though often in team competitions, only the Top 4 gets the spotlight, that doesn't take anything away from a Pro Tour Top 8 as far as I'm concerned. And as for Luis Scott-Vargas, I think he's made a Top 8 or two. I tried to think of a time as his Hall of Fame ring glittered in the sun.

This is the match-up of the Top 8. Pod and Affinity are the most-represented decks in the Top 8. This match could be a harbinger of the finals.

Sigrist felt great about the deck line-up. He was as confident as one can be going up against someone named Luis Scott-Vargas.

### Game One

[Darksteel Citadel](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503173724/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Darksteel+Citadel), [Memnite](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503173724/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Memnite), [Mox Opal](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503173724/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Mox+Opal), and [Etched Champion](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503173724/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Etched+Champion) were the turn-one plays from Sigrist. It was a decent start, I guess.

<table><tbody><tr><td>&#10;			<div>&#10;			<div><img alt="Mox Opal" src="http://gatherer.wizards.com/Handlers/Image.ashx?type=card&amp;name=Mox+Opal"></div>&#10;			</div>&#10;			</td>&#10;			<td>&#10;			<div>&#10;			<div><img alt="Darksteel Citadel" src="http://gatherer.wizards.com/Handlers/Image.ashx?type=card&amp;name=Darksteel+Citadel"></div>&#10;			</div>&#10;			</td>&#10;		</tr></tbody></table>

"That it?" Scott-Vargas stone faced. This was going to be tough for LSV.

Next turn Sigrist added a [Signal Pest](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503173724/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Signal+Pest) and beat to 17-20. In what seemed like the blink of an eye, Luis was down to 11. Though [Kitchen Finks](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503173724/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Kitchen+Finks) brought him back up to 13, he was still under pressure. Then [Blinkmoth Nexus](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503173724/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Blinkmoth+Nexus) and [Master of Etherium](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503173724/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Master+of+Etherium) joined the party. [Signal Pest](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503173724/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Signal+Pest) gladly boosted them and the [Etched Champion](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503173724/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Etched+Champion) during the next attack step. LSV sunk to 5.

He made a slight raspberry with his mouth when he drew his card for the turn. That might have well have been his concession.

Apparently, on the West Coast, "Thpth" is actually shorthand for "I declare my scoop phase."

### Michael Sigrist 1 – 0 Luis Scott-Vargas

![](https://i.ormos.uk/74/c0/74c0c0d6a31ddd1adeb718941823528e0b9ab4d5c90126b1b5063731b350680a.jpg)  
*Michael Sigrist* 

### Game Two

LSV went first this game, and cast [Overgrown Tomb](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503173724/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Overgrown+Tomb) and [Birds of Paradise](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503173724/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Birds+of+Paradise). This was as good a start as you can hope for. This allows for acceleration into the bigger spells and creatures that can impact the board.

But although Sigrist was a half-turn behind, he went [Blinkmoth Nexus](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503173724/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Blinkmoth+Nexus), [Mox Opal](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503173724/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Mox+Opal), [Ornithopter](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503173724/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Ornithopter), [Vault Skirge](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503173724/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Vault+Skirge), [Dismember](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503173724/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Dismember) the Birds. Then went [Springleaf Drum](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503173724/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Springleaf+Drum) and [Arcbound Ravager](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503173724/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Arcbound+Ravager). Taking the totals to 16-15 in LSV's favor. If you just walked in on the game, not only would you have thought Sigrist had went first, you probably would have thought Scott-Vargas showed up late. Despite being behind, the Hall-of-Famer remained jovial.

"Has that Ravager been played before?" Scott-Vargas said as he looked at the well-loved wear around the edges of the *Darksteel* standout.

Scott-Vargas's second-turn accelerator, [Noble Hierarch](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503173724/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Noble+Hierarch), stuck, and he used it to cast a [Birthing Pod](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503173724/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Birthing+Pod). Then in selflessly sacrificed itself to make a [Kataki, War's Wage](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503173724/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Kataki%2C+War%27s+Wage). This was usually very difficult to overcome, but the well-love Ravager made sure all extraneous artifacts were converted into +1/+1 counters.

Sigrist kept the [Arcbound Ravager](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503173724/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Arcbound+Ravager), [Vault Skirge](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503173724/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Vault+Skirge), [Blinkmoth Nexus](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503173724/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Blinkmoth+Nexus), and an Island. Before attackers he laid down a [Darksteel Citadel](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503173724/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Darksteel+Citadel) and cast a [Master of Etherium](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503173724/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Master+of+Etherium). This drew a wince from Luis. The Ravager had become a 4/4, and Luis, ever-conscious of his life total chump-blocked with his Kataki.

<table><tbody><tr><td>&#10;			<div>&#10;			<div><img alt="Arcbound Ravager" src="http://gatherer.wizards.com/Handlers/Image.ashx?type=card&amp;name=Arcbound+Ravager"></div>&#10;			</div>&#10;			</td>&#10;			<td>&#10;			<div>&#10;			<div><img alt="Master of Etherium" src="http://gatherer.wizards.com/Handlers/Image.ashx?type=card&amp;name=Master+of+Etherium"></div>&#10;			</div>&#10;			</td>&#10;		</tr></tbody></table>

"I couldn't afford my own Kataki," Luis said as he shipped the white creature to the bin. The life totals became 10-17. That [Vault Skirge](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503173724/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Vault+Skirge) was starting to pay for itself in life.

Scott-Vargas had an empty board save three lands and a [Birthing Pod](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503173724/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Birthing+Pod), and his opponent was down to just one card (probably because basically his entire hand had been on the battlefield since the first turn).

[Orzhov Pontiff](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503173724/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Orzhov+Pontiff) came down, then gave himself to the [Birthing Pod](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503173724/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Birthing+Pod), and Scott-Vargas birthed a [Linvala, Keeper of Silence](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503173724/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Linvala%2C+Keeper+of+Silence). Scott-Vargas was actually dead on board with all the artifact creatures, so his odd-looking play was made in the hopes that Sigrist would forget his [Vault Skirge](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503173724/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Vault+Skirge) was actually a 2/2 thanks to [Master of Etherium](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503173724/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Master+of+Etherium). If he could get the Affinity player to give up one of his creatures to the [Arcbound Ravager](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503173724/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Arcbound+Ravager) for free, he might survive.

Sigrist didn't bite. Luis ended his turn, then a few moments later extended his hand.

### Michael Sigrist has defeated Luis Scott-Vargas 2-0 to advance to the Semifinals!

### Jamie Parke (Tempo Twin) vs. Vipin Chackonal (Affinity)

Vipin Chackonal was extremely excited to make it here in the Top 8. Not only is this his biggest finish thus far, but today is his birthday!

"This is the best birthday present ever!" He was quite elated. But not so fast, Chackonal. I wouldn't be too happy to have to face down Jamie Parke. Maybe that name doesn't mean much to some of the younger of you, but Parke has put up Top 8s across three decades now.

His Tempo Twin deck can be a beast to handle, because it can always threaten to win at a moment's notice. Though less-suited to beat Affinity, Tempo Twin was made to delay the game just enough to eke out the win.

### Game One

[Memnite](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503173724/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Memnite), [Springleaf Drum](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503173724/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Springleaf+Drum), [Signal Pest](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503173724/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Signal+Pest), and [Inkmoth Nexus](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503173724/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Inkmoth+Nexus) hit the field for Vipin Chackonal on the first turn. Luis Scott-Vargas heard the slaps of cardboard onto the table from the other side of the Top 8 tables and said, "I don't know exactly what's in play, but it probably includes a [Mox Opal](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503173724/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Mox+Opal), maybe two."

<table><tbody><tr><td>&#10;			<div>&#10;			<div><img alt="Springleaf Drum" src="http://gatherer.wizards.com/Handlers/Image.ashx?type=card&amp;name=Springleaf+Drum"></div>&#10;			</div>&#10;			</td>&#10;			<td>&#10;			<div>&#10;			<div><img alt="Memnite" src="http://gatherer.wizards.com/Handlers/Image.ashx?type=card&amp;name=Memnite"></div>&#10;			</div>&#10;			</td>&#10;		</tr></tbody></table>

That was a heck of a turn one. But Jamie Parke was able to [Lightning Bolt](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503173724/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Lightning+Bolt) the [Signal Pest](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503173724/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Signal+Pest) to stop any real beats for at least the next turn. Chackonal followed with a Bolt-proof [Etched Champion](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503173724/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Etched+Champion) and brought the beats soon enough. Parke was behind on board, and also had to mulligan, so he was playing catch-up on two fronts.

A turn or so later, after a [Serum Visions](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503173724/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Serum+Visions), Chackonal cast an [Arcbound Ravager](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503173724/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Arcbound+Ravager) that was immediately Bolted as well (but it grew the Champion to a 3/3).

Parke was down to 9 life by the time he took his fourth turn. [Electrolyze](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503173724/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Electrolyze) took out all the non-[Etched Champion](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503173724/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Etched+Champion) creatures to maintain his life total as best he could. It was low, but at least it would stay the same. The fist part of Tempo Twin was working—it was delaying. Now the tough part—winning before you lose.

There was a rules question that gave Jamie an extra life. Chackonal meant to attack with his [Blinkmoth Nexus](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503173724/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Blinkmoth+Nexus) but had accidentally passed into the declare attackers phase. Chackonal was a jittery and excited at his first time under the lights. And this time, this mistake didn't matter.

Whether Parke was at 6 life or 7, Parked couldn't find an answer in time to stop the unkillable Champion.

### Vipin Chackonal 1 – 0 Jamie Parke

![](https://i.ormos.uk/b2/a3/b2a32f7b04d3bca1e11ebbd8261bde6f4215add13732713692c598bdfcd4c486.jpg)  
*Vipin Chackonal* 

### Game Two

Parke played a [Serum Visions](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503173724/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Serum+Visions) on his first turn, while Chackonal was a little more proactive—casting a [Blinkmoth Nexus](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503173724/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Blinkmoth+Nexus), [Ornithopter](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503173724/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Ornithopter), [Springleaf Drum](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503173724/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Springleaf+Drum), and [Vault Skirge](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503173724/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Vault+Skirge).

His second turn was worse than the first. Another [Ornithopter](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503173724/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Ornithopter) and [Cranial Plating](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503173724/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Cranial+Plating) allowed him to attack for six. It was 13-24 in Chackonal's favor on turn two. It looked like Chackonal was on his way to a quick 2-0 just like the Michael Sigrist made against Luis Scott-Vargas. But Parke had other plans.

He had the combo waiting in his hand, and all he had to do was live until turn four. If he did, he could cast [Deceiver Exarch](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503173724/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Deceiver+Exarch) and enchant it with [Splinter Twin](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503173724/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Splinter+Twin) to make the all-but-infinite combo of thousands upon thousands of attackers.

Chackonal tried to beat as fast as he could, but killing on turn three, on the draw, is quite the difficult task to complete. He couldn't do it. Though Parke was brought deep into single digits, on the end of Chackonal's third turn, Parke flashed out a [Deceiver Exarch](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503173724/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?&name=Deceiver%2BExarch), then calmly untapped and resolved his [Splinter Twin](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503173724/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Splinter+Twin) for the win.

### Vipin Chackonal 1 – 1 Jamie Parke

![](https://i.ormos.uk/02/56/025608ed12c0c9d089530c6685f732540bcde21580edbd2edd07df5299379471.jpg)  
*Jamie Parke* 

### Game Three

"You're on the play. That's bad for me." Those sound like famous last words out of Jamie Parke. In the first two turns, Chackonal had made two [Ornithopter](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503173724/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Ornithopter), two [Springleaf Drum](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503173724/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Springleaf+Drum)s, a [Vault Skirge](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503173724/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Vault+Skirge), and a [Blinkmoth Nexus](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503173724/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Blinkmoth+Nexus). Though lots of cards were on the battlefield, there weren't really any damage-dealers.

Parke thought he was just fine. He was sitting on [Anger of the Gods](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503173724/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Anger+of+the+Gods), and so he would just wait for the right moment to crush. But then his own dreams got crushed. On the third turn the big things came down. Both [Torpor Orb](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503173724/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Torpor+Orb) and [Thoughtseize](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503173724/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Thoughtseize) resolved. Parke slumped visibly in his chair when the [Thoughtseize](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503173724/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Thoughtseize) resolved.

Two [Deceiver Exarch](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503173724/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?&name=Deceiver%2BExarch), [Snapcaster Mage](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503173724/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Snapcaster+Mage), two land, [Anger of the Gods](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503173724/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Anger+of+the+Gods) and [Flame Slash](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503173724/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Flame+Slash). Parke dutifully notated what his opponent saw in addition to Chackonal marking it. This diligence would hopefully pay off later. But as for now, all of Parke's creatures were basically worthless and the [Anger of the Gods](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503173724/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Anger+of+the+Gods) was in his graveyard.

Parke was a little shaken, and was getting a little itchy. He cast the Snapcaster the following turn and tried to target his [Serum Visions](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503173724/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Serum+Visions) in the graveyard, but Chackonal informed him that [Torpor Orb](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503173724/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Torpor+Orb) stopped that. Parke passed the turn.

![Etched Champion](http://gatherer.wizards.com/Handlers/Image.ashx?type=card&name=Etched+Champion)

Chackonal had a stranglehold on the game, but his clock was very, very slow. Parke's life total went to 17, then 16, then 13. Parke, trying to stem the inexorable bleed, cast [Deceiver Exarch](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503173724/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Deceiver+Exarch) as a [Horned Turtle](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503173724/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Horned+Turtle). It was a sad day for the New Yorker. Soon the [Etched Champion](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503173724/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Etched+Champion)s came down and the [Blinkmoth Nexus](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503173724/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Blinkmoth+Nexus) animated. Affinity started taking bigger chunks of Parke's life than he could handle.

On the last possible turn, Parke drew an [Ancient Grudge](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503173724/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Ancient+Grudge) and perked up in his seat. He could knocked out [Torpor Orb](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503173724/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Torpor+Orb), and if he drew either [Kiki-Jiki, Mirror Breaker](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503173724/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Kiki-Jiki%2C+Mirror+Breaker) or [Splinter Twin](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503173724/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Splinter+Twin), he could still win. And even in the worst-case scenario, he could kill both the [Blinkmoth Nexus](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503173724/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Blinkmoth+Nexus) when the animated and have a shot. But those plans were not meant to be.

Before animating either of the lands, Chackonal cast a [Spellskite](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503173724/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Spellskite). This would divert Parke's [Ancient Grudge](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503173724/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Ancient+Grudge) either time he tried to cast it. So both potential plans were basically for naught. Parke looked around the board and realized there was nothing he could do.

He extended his hand to Chackonal.

### Vipin Chackonal, the birthday boy, advances to the semifinals over Jamie Parke 2-1!

These matches were covered on video as well. If you missed the live action, videos will be up on the Star City Games YouTube channel a week after the Grand Prix.

* * *

## Quarterfinals - Josh McClain (Melira Pod) vs. Oscar Jones (Melira Pod)

*by Adam Styborski*

 

The Melira Pod master had done it again. After winning Grand Prix Detroit 2013 with his deck, number 19-ranked Josh McClain ran deep into Pro Tour *Born of the Gods* and was poised to run it through for a another Grand Prix victory here in Richmond.

Oscar Jones bore experience beyond his youthful years, having experience with prize finishes at several Grand Prix before. Already qualified for Pro Tour Journey Into Nyx with his Top 8 appearance here, Jones' challenge was something different: Defeat a current master of the deck he himself was playing.

Each players' confidence was clear with how quickly they handled their decks, a sure sign of knowing every one of it's silver bullet cards by heart. The question would be who would find them first.

The first game found Jones led off with early creatures, [Murderous Redcap](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503173724/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Murderous+Redcap) and [Orzhov Pontiff](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503173724/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Orzhov+Pontiff), but McClain found the first [Birthing Pod](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503173724/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Birthing+Pod). From a low 11 life McClain tried to fight back, with a [Birds of Paradise](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503173724/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Birds+of+Paradise) transforming into [Wall of Roots](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503173724/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Wall+of+Roots), but Jones' [Abrupt Decay](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503173724/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Abrupt+Decay) kept blockers out of the way. With [Gavony Township](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503173724/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Gavony+Township) to knock McClain down to just 2 life, McClain was out of outs.

![](https://i.ormos.uk/ae/33/ae33adde74c95b8e5525fc602f4b138b375b0d7320c0437c5cb49084ba4410f7.jpg)  
*Jones, like McClain, didn't need to look at decklists between games. He knew his Melira Pod deck.* 

The second game was a flipped situation, with McClain drawing down a quick army of [Qasali Pridemage](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503173724/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Qasali+Pridemage), [Scavenging Ooze](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503173724/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Scavenging+Ooze), and [Kitchen Finks](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503173724/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Kitchen+Finks). Using [Dismember](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503173724/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Dismember) and a green mana, McClain protected his Ooze from Jones' [Murderous Redcap](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503173724/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Murderous+Redcap)s. Attacking Jones to 8 life, and adding a second [Scavenging Ooze](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503173724/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Scavenging+Ooze) to keep persist shenanigans locked out, Jones played the only card that could unfreeze his combos: [Linvala, Keeper of Silence](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503173724/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Linvala%2C+Keeper+of+Silence). While his Angel unlocked persist it came too late to stop McClain's army.

"So far, the card [Birthing Pod](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503173724/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Birthing+Pod) has not won a game," McClain chuckled upon tying it up.

"Yeah, just idiot creatures in our decks," Jones agreed, sharing a quick laugh.

![](https://i.ormos.uk/50/f7/50f7176dd4b602704e299108a806fd3c6177bae93b7f32e41ff2a1f9d894cac6.jpg)  
*McClain was more than familiar with "the beatdown plan" for Melria Pod.* 

The third game featured equally quick starts: Jones with Noble Heirarch and [Kitchen Finks](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503173724/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Kitchen+Finks), McClain with his own Hierarch and [Qasali Pridemage](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503173724/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Qasali+Pridemage). While McClain valiantly killed every one-drop mana generator Jones played, the younger player always had another ready, even using [Eternal Witness](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503173724/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Eternal+Witness) to return [Noble Hierarch](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503173724/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Noble+Hierarch) for another go.

Tapping out exactly his mana and creatures for [Chord of Calling](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503173724/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Chord+of+Calling), [Orzhov Pontiff](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503173724/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Orzhov+Pontiff) reset the mana makers on McClain's side to leave him with just two mana available. Jones used a [Thoughtseize](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503173724/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Thoughtseize) to find [Birthing Pod](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503173724/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Birthing+Pod), [Murderous Redcap](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503173724/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Murderous+Redcap), and [Harmonic Sliver](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503173724/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Harmonic+Sliver) in McClain's hand, and chose to strip away the potent combo piece. It cost him falling to 5 life but it didn't matter.

A second [Chord of Calling](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503173724/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Chord+of+Calling) found [Murderous Redcap](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503173724/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Murderous+Redcap) for Jones, and with a [Birthing Pod](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503173724/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Birthing+Pod) on his turn he took command of the game by adding [Melira, Sylvok Outcast](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503173724/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Melira%2C+Sylvok+Outcast) to the battlefield to join [Viscera Seer](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503173724/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Viscera+Seer). While an arbitrary amount of life was at his fingertips, he needed to be sure to deal lethal damage to McClain. After [Thoughtseize](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503173724/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Thoughtseize) found the path clear, [Kitchen Finks](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503173724/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Kitchen+Finks) became [Murderous Redcap](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503173724/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Murderous+Redcap) to seal the deal for Jones.

"I needed him to not draw a land the turn he played [Orzhov Pontiff](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503173724/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Orzhov+Pontiff), and if I had drawn a land I could have played my [Birthing Pod](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503173724/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Birthing+Pod)," McClain lamented. While the Pod master fell short of the repeat, perhaps the future of the format rests in young Jones' hands.

### Oscar Jones defeated Josh McClain, 2-1.

* * *

## Quarterfinals: Ben Friedman (Melira Pod) vs. Brian Liu (Kiki Pod)

*by Reuben Bressler*

 

One deck has proven itself to be enemy number one of this event: Melira Pod. Originally a combo-centric deck focused on achieving the combo of [Viscera Seer](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503173724/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Viscera+Seer); [Melira, Sylvok Outcast](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503173724/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Melira%2C+Sylvok+Outcast); and either [Kitchen Finks](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503173724/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Kitchen+Finks) or [Murderous Redcap](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503173724/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Murderous+Redcap) has become less about trying to combo quickly and more about controlling the ground game, grinding little advantages and using [Gavony Township](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503173724/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Gavony+Township) to create an army out of nowhere.

Ben Friedman is one of the players in this Top 8 playing the Junk-colored menace. Ben is no stranger to the top 8 stage, with two Grand Prix top 8s and a slew of other high tournament finishes already under his belt.

While his opponent may have less knockout round experience, Brian Liu is nonetheless ready by playing [Birthing Pod](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503173724/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Birthing+Pod). Liu is using the slightly older and more combo-centric version, Kiki Pod, which uses the power utilized by many Twin decks of [Deceiver Exarch](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503173724/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Deceiver+Exarch) and [Kiki-Jiki, Mirror Breaker](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503173724/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Kiki-Jiki%2C+Mirror+Breaker).

Liu is a road warrior: He drove here with a few of his buddies all the way from Toronto, Canada. The twelve hour drive paid off immediately, since he walked in the door and won the first Last Chance Grand Prix Trial he entered. He then parlayed those byes into an identical 13-1-1 record.

As the players shuffled up, the competitors commiserated about the incredible amount of [Birthing Pod](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503173724/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Birthing+Pod) decks in the top 8, and the interesting one-ofs in each deck like [Restoration Angel](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503173724/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Restoration+Angel) and [Big Game Hunter](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503173724/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Big+Game+Hunter). Liu also had a quick comment about Friedman's numbers on his deck registration sheet.

"I see you only have one Redcap..."

"Yeah, I don't know why people keep insisting on two of that. That card is uncastable!" Friedman explained.

![](https://i.ormos.uk/5c/c0/5cc085e34a41276dc2d0da5e8940c89321462b5e06336e6848f76dcabd380351.jpg)  
*Ben Friedman was thrilled to find himself in his third Grand Prix Top 8.* 

Once Friedman kept his six, he led things off with [Noble Hierarch](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503173724/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Noble+Hierarch). Liu fetched and shocked himself with [Stomping Ground](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503173724/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Stomping+Ground) to play a [Noble Hierarch](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503173724/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Noble+Hierarch) of his own. Friedman's turn two was not particularly impressive, missing a land drop and dropping another Hierarch. Liu's was better, dropping [Wall of Roots](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503173724/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Wall+of+Roots) as well as [Birds of Paradise](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503173724/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Birds+of+Paradise) in addition to [Misty Rainforest](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503173724/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Misty+Rainforest).

Friedman used his turn three to play [Spellskite](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503173724/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Spellskite). Liu dropped [Linvala, Keeper of Silence](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503173724/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Linvala%2C+Keeper+of+Silence) on his main phase, which stymied Ben's [Noble Hierarch](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503173724/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Noble+Hierarch)s. Left with naught but a Forest for mana at this point, Liu brought the beats. Linvala picked up an exalted trigger thanks to Liu's own [Noble Hierarch](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503173724/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Noble+Hierarch). When Ben finally found a land to try to knock off [Wall of Roots](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503173724/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Wall+of+Roots) with [Abrupt Decay](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503173724/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Abrupt+Decay), Brain was ready and waiting with [Restoration Angel](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503173724/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Restoration+Angel). A turn later [Chord of Calling](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503173724/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Chord+of+Calling) for five found [Restoration Angel](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503173724/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Restoration+Angel)'s good friend [Kiki-Jiki, Mirror Breaker](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503173724/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Kiki-Jiki%2C+Mirror+Breaker), and as soon as Liu began making an arbitrarily large number of 3/4s with the goblin legend Friedman scooped up his cards and headed for his sideboard.

"This is my second Pod matchup; the first one was a Kiki Pod mirror. This is actually the first time I've played Melira Pod in this event." Liu said.

Friedman didn't have a reply.

![](https://i.ormos.uk/da/f2/daf2121b57b80f766f46815cb8f685cd19500b396d8dc06f3cfc9eb9c752598b.jpg)  
*Facing Melira Pod for the first time in the tournament, Brian Liu seemed prepared.* 

To start the second game, Friedman cast [Thoughtseize](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503173724/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Thoughtseize) on turn two and took [Linvala, Keeper of Silence](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503173724/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Linvala%2C+Keeper+of+Silence) from Liu's grip.

From [Thoughtseize](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503173724/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Thoughtseize) we knew that [Deceiver Exarch](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503173724/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Deceiver+Exarch) was going to be Liu's play, and it attempted to tap down [Birds of Paradise](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503173724/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Birds+of+Paradise). Friedman went to his turn and made his third land drop before adding [Birthing Pod](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503173724/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Birthing+Pod) to his board, paying two life to do so. Liu, now facing a potential waterfall of card advantage thanks to the green artifact, added [Birds of Paradise](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503173724/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Birds+of+Paradise) number two.

Friedman used the Pod to turn the Birds into a [Wall of Roots](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503173724/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Wall+of+Roots): Not the explosive starts from Melira Pod he hoped. Liu dropped [Noble Hierarch](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503173724/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Noble+Hierarch) before using [Chord of Calling](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503173724/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Chord+of+Calling) to search out [Qasali Pridemage](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503173724/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Qasali+Pridemage) to deal with [Birthing Pod](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503173724/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Birthing+Pod).

![Birthing Pod](http://gatherer.wizards.com/Handlers/Image.ashx?type=card&name=Birthing+Pod)

Liu was still flush with gas, dropping [Kitchen Finks](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503173724/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Kitchen+Finks) as the sixth creature on his side of things. [Scavenging Ooze](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503173724/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Scavenging+Ooze) ate both of the creatures in the graveyard before Friedman untapped, and then the now 4/4 Ooze was able to rumble in for some damage... or it would have had it not been sent on the [Path to Exile](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503173724/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Path+to+Exile).

Then Liu dropped [Birthing Pod](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503173724/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Birthing+Pod), which he used the Pod, sacrificing [Deceiver Exarch](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503173724/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Deceiver+Exarch) to find [Restoration Angel](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503173724/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Restoration+Angel). Friedman used the [Gavony Township](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503173724/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Gavony+Township) at end of turn to make his team slightly larger, but with no army only the Redcap and the [Wall of Roots](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503173724/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Wall+of+Roots) grew thanks to the activation. Again a pair of [Restoration Angel](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503173724/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Restoration+Angel)s came into the red zone, and Friedman fell to 5. Friedman found no help on top on his turn, and Liu's Ooze became Titan-sized on the end step. There was little Friedman could do. With a shake of his head, Friedman conceded the match.

"You drew a lot of lands there."

"I played a land light strategy game 1 and a land heavy one game 2."

"...And I topdecked two Pods!"

Friedman shrugged. "Sometimes they run hotter than you!"

### Brian Liu defeats Ben Friedman, 2-0.

* * *

## Semifinals - Vipin Chackonal (Affinity) vs. Michael Sigrist (Affinity)

*by Marc Calderaro*

 

Here was the blink-and-you'll-miss-it-Affinity mirror match. Meanwhile in the other Semifinals match, there were two Pod decks. So the Top 4 matchups could be thought of as the primary race to see which deck would be a better representative in general-election face-off of Affinity vs. Pod. Though the two Affinity player's decks are extremely similar, Vipin Chackonal's has a few more non-artifact spells in extra [Thoughtcast](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503173724/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Thoughtcast) and [Galvanic Blast](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503173724/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Galvanic+Blast). This meant the deck could go a little longer, but was less explosive than Michael Sigrist's deck.

Keep in mind we're really splitting hairs here. The decks are only a few cards apart.

The two shuffled up for the first game, and I prepared for a blisteringly fast match.

### Game One

Michael Sigrist went first, and, subverting all expectation, started very slowly. He just laid a land and passed the turn. What? Is this even Affinity? Luckily Chackonal satiated my need for speed by going [Darksteel Citadel](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503173724/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Darksteel+Citadel), [Mox Opal](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503173724/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Mox+Opal), [Signal Pest](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503173724/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Signal+Pest), [Springleaf Drum](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503173724/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Springleaf+Drum), [Memnite](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503173724/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Memnite), and [Cranial Plating](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503173724/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Cranial+Plating) in the first two turns. Phew.

Sigrist cast a [Steel Overseer](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503173724/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Steel+Overseer) and accepted the trade Chackonal offered him when a plated-up [Memnite](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503173724/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Memnite) marched into the red zone. Though the Overseer was often the key to the match, the [Cranial Plating](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503173724/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Cranial+Plating) required Sigrist to save all the damage he could and hope to attrition out Chackonal. However, it didn't look like Chackonal was planning on slowing up.

![](https://i.ormos.uk/fa/88/fa88419a8fbbf272399e2b5e50c815b2852f40b0aa71cb43abc5681eb139e508.jpg)  
*Michael Sigrist* 

Chackonal continued to gas out with [Arcbound Ravager](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503173724/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Arcbound+Ravager) and [Ornithopter](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503173724/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Ornithopter). He equipped the [Signal Pest](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503173724/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Signal+Pest) with the [Cranial Plating](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503173724/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Cranial+Plating) and offered second trade with [Vault Skirge](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503173724/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Vault+Skirge). It was again accepted.

The life totals were still near 20 apiece, but the board state was changing in flurries. When Sigrist resolved his own [Cranial Plating](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503173724/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Cranial+Plating) he began to even out the board. Granted, Chackonal's newly equipped Ravager was a 7/1 rather than a 3/1, but still, to the untrained eye it looked even.

Sigrist's hand was all-but empty when a [Thoughtcast](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503173724/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Thoughtcast) gave him another [Steel Overseer](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503173724/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Steel+Overseer) and a second [Cranial Plating](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503173724/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Cranial+Plating). Combined with his second [Arcbound Ravager](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503173724/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Arcbound+Ravager), the counting game had begun. Chackonal, an accountant, was the first player to physically count out potential damage he was calculating in his brain. He pointed his finger over and over towards the table as if he was using an invisible Morse code machine.

He eventually cast [Arcbound Ravager](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503173724/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Arcbound+Ravager) #2 pre-combat and swung in. Sigrist was at 11. He made all the blocks he could. But then the shenanigans started.

Even more "flurrious" than the flurries before, artifacts flew around the battlefield and graveyard. Thanks to [Arcbound Ravager](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503173724/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Arcbound+Ravager), counters moved freely to wherever they would deal the most damage. Eventually, an unblocked, flying artifact was at 11 power. And that was that. It turned out here, the place where counters would deal the most damage was in Sigrist's piehole.

### Vipin Chackonal 1 – 0 Michael Sigrist

![](https://i.ormos.uk/9f/c4/9fc4f05f1dafa76cbb5205eb761fa7174adf61234f6060fbee403d08a4c01de7.jpg)  
*Vipin Chackonal* 

### Game Two

Sigrist had a more Affinity-like first turn this game, with a [Darksteel Citadel](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503173724/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Darksteel+Citadel), [Ornithopter](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503173724/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Ornithopter), and a [Signal Pest](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503173724/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Signal+Pest). Not overly explosive, but at least I could tell it was Affinity. Chackonal answered in kind. However, unlike Sigrist, Chackonal added and equipped a [Cranial Plating](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503173724/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Cranial+Plating). This was a very big difference and meant [Memnite](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503173724/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Memnite) swung for 8 (Plating giving bonus for [Ornithopter](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503173724/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Ornithopter), [Signal Pest](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503173724/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Signal+Pest), [Memnite](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503173724/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Memnite), [Mox Opal](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503173724/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Mox+Opal) and [Darksteel Citadel](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503173724/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Darksteel+Citadel)).

Sigrist was back on the defensive. He cast an [Arcbound Ravager](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503173724/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Arcbound+Ravager) and passed, trying to maintain the life totals at 12-20. Chackonal gladly continue his offensive assault, moving the Plating to the 0/2 flyer. Sigrist had a [Galvanic Blast](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503173724/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Galvanic+Blast) in hand but no source of red mana, and considered whether he should chump block with his own [Ornithopter](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503173724/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Ornithopter). He declined and sunk to 4 life. Odd isn't it, that a free 0/2 can deal so much damage? Such is the wonders of synergy.

![Master of Etherium](http://gatherer.wizards.com/Handlers/Image.ashx?type=card&name=Master+of+Etherium)

Sigrist had some heavy hitters in his hand: [Master of Etherium](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503173724/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Master+of+Etherium), [Steel Overseer](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503173724/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Steel+Overseer) and a second Ravager, but Chackonal wasn't giving him much time to cast them. [Glimmervoid](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503173724/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Glimmervoid) came down, allowing Sigrist to keep open the precious red to cast a [Galvanic Blast](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503173724/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Galvanic+Blast). He still couldn't attack, but two [Signal Pest](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503173724/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Signal+Pest), two [Arcbound Ravager](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503173724/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Arcbound+Ravager)s, an [Ornithopter](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503173724/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Ornithopter) and a Blast in hand made for a decent defense.

But just like last time, it still wasn't enough. Sigrist could only block so much. And just the like dreaded Affinity deck during *Mirrodin* Standard, with enough sacrificing, any unblocked artifact attacker can turn into a giant. And coincidentally enough, that giant always seems to be exactly the size of the opponent's remaining life total.

Sigrist lost his remaining life and extended his hand to Vipin Chackonal.

### Vipin Chackonal—first time Top 8er and birthday boy—is heading to the finals, 2-0 over Michael Sigrist.

This match was covered on video as well. If you missed the live action, the video will be up on the Star City Games YouTube channel a week after the Grand Prix.

* * *

## Semifinals - Brian Liu (Kiki Pod) vs. Oscar Jones (Melira Pod)

*by Adam Styborski*

 

Which Pod is the best Pod? Melira Pod was out in much greater numbers than Kiki Pod this weekend, but from where Brian Liu sit it didn't matter: He had already slain Ben Friedman's Melira Pod.

While it wasn't a Melira Pod mirror match as when he faced down Josh McClain, Oscar Jones was more than ready to do it all over again against Liu's Kiki Pod choice. Both players were anxious to keep things moving.

![](https://i.ormos.uk/cd/23/cd23034d7fe527932e356f7b7b32eca4e36a95a12ce4f78ed2fb6d32b4cb3f79.jpg)  
*Jones looked prepared against another type of Pod deck.* 

With an early [Spellskite](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503173724/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Spellskite), Jones looked to lock away some of what Liu might do, but [Chord of Calling](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503173724/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Chord+of+Calling) for [Linvala, Keeper of Silence](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503173724/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Linvala%2C+Keeper+of+Silence) broke the chains. Liu had plans of his own.

Undaunted, Jones played and used [Birthing Pod](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503173724/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Birthing+Pod) to find a Linvala of his own and maintain the stalemate parity. [Chord of Calling](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503173724/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Chord+of+Calling) again from Liu found [Zealous Conscripts](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503173724/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Zealous+Conscripts) to take, and kill, the newest Angel. Jones dug through his deck with [Birthing Pod](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503173724/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Birthing+Pod) again, but Jones realized there wasn't any way to stop Liu from finishing him off.

The second game started with Jones seeing Liu's hand off [Thoughtseize](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503173724/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Thoughtseize) – removing the sideboarded [Combust](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503173724/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Combust) he found there – and followed it up with both [Birthing Pod](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503173724/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Birthing+Pod) and [Linvala, Keeper of Silence](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503173724/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Linvala%2C+Keeper+of+Silence). Then the waves of removal jousting began: Jones' [Dismember](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503173724/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Dismember) for [Deceiver Exarch](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503173724/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Deceiver+Exarch) in response to [Restoration Angel](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503173724/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Restoration+Angel), then Liu's [Combust](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503173724/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Combust) for Linvala in response to [Spellskite](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503173724/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Spellskite).

It left [Restoration Angel](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503173724/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Restoration+Angel) free to attack, pressuring Jones to 7 life.

![](https://i.ormos.uk/66/6d/666dda59649db6a5710af9f423afe8934eafe185ca3a29e9039f07958d024264.jpg)  
*At every turn, Liu seemed to have exactly what he needed against Jones.* 

[Birthing Pod](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503173724/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Birthing+Pod) transformed Jones' [Spellskite](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503173724/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Spellskite) into [Eternal Witness](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503173724/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Eternal+Witness) and [Linvala, Keeper of Silence](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503173724/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Linvala%2C+Keeper+of+Silence) all over again, but Liu's [Zealous Conscripts](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503173724/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Zealous+Conscripts) were there to steal the Angel and end Jones' Top 8 run.

"You have no burn, right?" Jones asked, knowing his opponent's decklist.

"Yeah."

"I could just take [Restoration Angel](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503173724/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Restoration+Angel) and next turn get stuff. Did you top deck that guy?" Jones followed up, referring to [Zealous Conscripts](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503173724/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Zealous+Conscripts).

"No, I had him."

Jones just shook his head. The speed in which Liu clinched his finals appearance left no doubt in Jones' mind.

### Brian Liu defeated Oscar Jones, 2-1.

* * *

## Finals - Vipin Chackonal (Affinity) vs. Brian Liu (Kiki Pod)

*by Adam Styborski*

 

Could the birthday boy unwrap one of the sweetest presents in **Magic**? It's a question Vipin Chackonal wanted to answer in the affirmative. With his first deep Grand Prix run, on his birthday no less, Chackonal was one match away from an achievement perhaps no one else has claimed: Find a Grand Prix victory on his or her birthday.

Brian Liu would not be an easy opponent. After slaying the monstrous Melira Pod deck twice in back-to-back matches, the last remaining contender was one that Liu surely saw over the event: Affinity. The most popular deck in the room coming into Day 2, Liu was determined to break down the last machinations standing in his way.

![Linvala, Keeper of Silence](http://gatherer.wizards.com/Handlers/Image.ashx?type=card&name=Linvala%2C+Keeper+of+Silence)

Chackonal's artifacts flooded the battlefield in Game 1, with [Signal Pest](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503173724/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Signal+Pest), [Cranial Plating](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503173724/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Cranial+Plating), [Ornithopter](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503173724/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Ornithopter), [Springleaf Drum](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503173724/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Springleaf+Drum), [Inkmoth Nexus](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503173724/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Inkmoth+Nexus), and [Blinkmoth Nexus](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503173724/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Blinkmoth+Nexus) all by his second turn. Liu's was equally impressive with a [Noble Hierarch](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503173724/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Noble+Hierarch) into second turn [Birthing Pod](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503173724/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Birthing+Pod).

Chackonal then activiated his [Inkmoth Nexus](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503173724/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Inkmoth+Nexus) to equip [Cranial Plating](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503173724/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Cranial+Plating), dealing 8 poison damage. Three turns down in the game. Liu thought for a moment before summoning [Linvala, Keeper of Silence](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503173724/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Linvala%2C+Keeper+of+Silence) – a creature with flying to ostensibly block.

Vipin planned his attack carefully, summoning [Vault Skirge](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503173724/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Vault+Skirge) before using [Ornithopter](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503173724/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Ornithopter) with [Cranial Plating](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503173724/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Cranial+Plating) to attack for 8 damage. That put Liu at 4 life and 8 poison counters. Four turns of uncontested Affinity, and Liu could only consider his options and pass the turn back. When Chackonal attacked, Liu used [Restoration Angel](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503173724/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Restoration+Angel) to play a second flying blocker, but there was just enough mana to move [Cranial Plating](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503173724/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Cranial+Plating) to the unblocked Vault *Scourge*.

Of course, Liu missed that using Pod on Linvala for [Kiki-Jiki, Mirror Breaker](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503173724/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Kiki-Jiki%2C+Mirror+Breaker) to copy [Noble Hierarch](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503173724/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Noble+Hierarch) then cast [Restoration Angel](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503173724/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Restoration+Angel) would have let him win on his turn.

Five turns. Affinity doesn't turn down a gift like that.

![](https://i.ormos.uk/b3/47/b347a32bb2f0b2ea8ceff8a89bb9a42b06156deaf3101b89d4f686d1e47f66f5.jpg)  
*Chackonal was joyous to make it as far as the finals.* 

The second game started similarly, with [Inkmoth Nexus](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503173724/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Inkmoth+Nexus), [Blinkmoth Nexus](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503173724/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Blinkmoth+Nexus), [Arcbound Ravager](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503173724/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Arcbound+Ravager), [Springleaf Drum](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503173724/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Springleaf+Drum), and [Vault Skirge](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503173724/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Vault+Skirge) all in play on Chackonal's second turn. This time, Liu had [Spellskite](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503173724/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Spellskite) to curb any modular options, and hitting four mana on turn four meant he also had the threat of [Restoration Angel](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503173724/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Restoration+Angel) active. Chackonal found himself without a clear attack and only another [Vault Skirge](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503173724/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Vault+Skirge) to add.

At the end of Chackonal's turn, Liu went for [Restoration Angel](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503173724/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Restoration+Angel) but the opportunity was open for [Galvanic Blast](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503173724/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Galvanic+Blast) to handle [Spellskite](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503173724/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Spellskite). A [Kiki-Jiki, Mirror Breaker](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503173724/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Kiki-Jiki%2C+Mirror+Breaker) off the top for Liu would end the game, but [Birthing Pod](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503173724/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Birthing+Pod) was a close second as [Wall of Roots](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503173724/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Wall+of+Roots) transformed into [Deceiver Exarch](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503173724/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Deceiver+Exarch), which untapped the Pod and transformed Linvala into Kiki-Jiki.

"I had to go for the combo. I couldn't play around another [Galvanic Blast](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503173724/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Galvanic+Blast) – which could have been the last card in \[Jones'\] hand. [Spellskite](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503173724/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Spellskite) was holding it together," Liu said before looking to Chackonal. "So you had a pretty poor hand?"

"Yeah." Chackonal just nodded and shrugged.

"All the marbles for the match are in my sideboard," Liu said.

"So are mine," Chackonal agreed.

"But mine are pretty powerful." Liu was unwittingly prescient with his boast.

![](https://i.ormos.uk/d7/12/d712d57fa0d33437bb4f4da0d0579c14562d4f48dfff7d13fa43e568fb9f82e5.jpg)  
*Liu laid our the future before anyone knew what happened.* 

The third, final game put Chackonal on the play again but with a mulligan. [Vault Skirge](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503173724/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Vault+Skirge) off an Island, then another [Vault Skirge](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503173724/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Vault+Skirge), [Mox Opal](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503173724/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Mox+Opal), and [Signal Pest](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503173724/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Signal+Pest) was a step slower than his blistering first game. This time Liu played the "fill the battlefield" style of Affinity, chaining [Wall of Roots](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503173724/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Wall+of+Roots) into another [Wall of Roots](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503173724/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Wall+of+Roots) and [Birds of Paradise](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503173724/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Birds+of+Paradise) all from a starting three mana.

Despite falling to 14 on the next attack Liu seemed unphased. [Shatterstorm](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503173724/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Shatterstorm) on his turn was the reason why, and Chackonal was left with just an Island. Unknown to Chackonal, Liu had two [Chord of Calling](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503173724/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Chord+of+Calling) in his hand. At the end of the turn, Liu used on to grand Restoration Angle, then untapped with the other for [Kiki-Jiki, Mirror Breaker](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503173724/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Kiki-Jiki%2C+Mirror+Breaker).

Chackonal smiled and extended his hand, unable to open the ultimate birthday gift. "Congratulations," he said.

Liu smiled back, and the cheer from his friends hanging above was all you needed to hear to know it was over.

"I saw two red sources and [Shatterstorm](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503173724/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Shatterstorm). I couldn't not keep." Liu beamed.

### Brian Liu defeated Vipin Chackonal, 2-0

* * *

## Top 5 Cards

*by Marc Calderaro*

 

 

![Scapeshift](http://gatherer.wizards.com/Handlers/Image.ashx?type=card&name=Scapeshift)

 

### 5\. [Scapeshift](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503173724/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Scapeshift)

It would be easy to overlook this four-mana green Sorcery. It gets lost in the shuffle of the Pods, the Twins, and all those robots. But this little guy refuses to quit. If you look at the archetypes of the undefeated decks from Day 1, [Scapeshift](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503173724/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Scapeshift) put 2 players (out of 12) in the X-0 bracket, but represented significantly smaller portion of the Day 2 metagame. To compare, the other deck that put up two undefeated players was Merfolk, and it had 21 pilots; [Scapeshift](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503173724/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Scapeshift) had 7. One of the two pilots, Andrew Calderon, was the last undefeated player at 13-0 in a 4,300-person tournament.

The Tempo-Combo deck that could (no, not Tempo-Twin) might be a little better positioned in Modern than people expect—especially because all the heat is on those three decks above.

 

![Spellskite](http://gatherer.wizards.com/Handlers/Image.ashx?type=card&name=Spellskite)

 

### 4\. [Spellskite](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503173724/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Spellskite)

This card is doing double duty here. Firstly, it's protection in the [Splinter Twin](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503173724/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Splinter+Twin) decks. Top 8 finisher Jamie Parke had two in his main deck. If you cast a [Spellskite](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503173724/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Spellskite) early, you can more confidently jam the combo out there if you need to. Sure, maybe your opponent has two [Path to Exile](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503173724/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Path+to+Exile)s and you're boned. But maybe they only have one. And four toughness is the magic number that survives the format's most common removal spell, [Lightning Bolt](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503173724/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Lightning+Bolt).

Secondly, it's a necessary card in the sideboard of many decks—and often against [Splinter Twin](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503173724/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Splinter+Twin) itself. It's best out of proactive decks that don't want to be bogged down by pesky spot removal. Hall of Fame member Zvi Mowshowitz noted that it was the best card in his sideboard—saying that it blanks decks like Hexproof Auras by requiring them to target your creature with all those awesome Auras.

The card came back to bite Parke in the Top 8. Vipin Chackonal used it out of his Affinity sideboard to protect [Torpor Orb](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503173724/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Torpor+Orb) during his march to the finals. If your opponents want to help their board, why don't you let them help yours instead?

 

![Thoughtseize](http://gatherer.wizards.com/Handlers/Image.ashx?type=card&name=Thoughtseize)

 

### 3\. [Thoughtseize](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503173724/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Thoughtseize)

In a field that has seen so many archetypes near the top tables, you need a sideboard card that can answer anything. [Thoughtseize](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503173724/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Thoughtseize), for a mere two life, can answer anything before it becomes a problem. Named by Quarterfinalist Luis Scott-Vargas and Finalist Vipin Chackonal as the best card in their sideboards, [Thoughtseize](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503173724/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Thoughtseize) is a one-mana response to the most powerful cards Modern can muster. And when people are trying to do unfair things, [Thoughtseize](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503173724/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Thoughtseize) can bring them back down to earth.

In the words of No. 8 Josh Utter-Leyton, "It forces uninteractive decks interact with you."

 

![Arcbound Ravager](http://gatherer.wizards.com/Handlers/Image.ashx?type=card&name=Arcbound+Ravager)

 

### 2\. [Arcbound Ravager](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503173724/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Arcbound+Ravager)

This card has not changed one bit, has it? The damage-stacking rules have changed around it, but Ravager doesn't care. It just does what it wants, and it does it well. You can pick many cards from the Affinity deck to highlight the powerful synergy of the deck, but the more Affinity gets played, the better [Arcbound Ravager](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503173724/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Arcbound+Ravager) becomes. Eventual finalist Vipin Chackonal leaned on it hard in the mirror match, allowing him to always trade his creatures profitably, netting a few +1/+1 counters for his trouble. And in the counting war that is the Affinity mirror match, "a few" is all it takes.

Additionally, [Arcbound Ravager](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503173724/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Arcbound+Ravager) is the deck's best card to fight against [Kataki, War's Wage](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503173724/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Kataki%2C+War%27s+Wage) from decks like Melira Pod. In the Quarters, Michael Sigrist used Arcbound to avoid paying upkeep costs for artifacts like [Ornithopter](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503173724/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Ornithopter) and [Signal Pest](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503173724/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Signal+Pest), while converting them into the valuable creature-pumping resources.

The game may have changed, but Ravager still knows all the rules.

 

![Shatterstorm](http://gatherer.wizards.com/Handlers/Image.ashx?type=card&name=Shatterstorm)

 

### 1\. [Shatterstorm](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503173724/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Shatterstorm)

"I told you to play that card!" A friend of winner Brian Liu called out the proclamation as he won the decisive third game of the finals. Having the right tools for the right decks was a theme players kept coming back to on Sunday, and [Shatterstorm](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503173724/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Shatterstorm) shouts what it's meant for: Affinity.

Working with a slower, six-card start, Brian Liu rendered Vipin Chackonal helpless with his potent artifact destruction. With only an Island left to worry about, Liu's victory lap of turns was a leisurely stroll.

That's what the right card in the sideboard can do for you, even if your friend had to convince you upfront.

* * *
